SARS-CoV-2 和 H3N2 流感的适应度变化

Fitness flux in SARS-CoV-2 and influenza H3N2
作者： Trevor Bedford
摘要中文翻译病毒适应的节奏通常是通过 dN/dS 等指标从 突变 的组成中间接读取的。在这里,我们直接根据变异频率的动态来测量它,其中我们使用多项式逻辑回归来估计每个共同循环变异的适合度。我们汇总这些估计值,得出平均人口适应度的变化率,称为适应度通量。追踪 SARS-CoV-2 从出现以来的情况,我们发现它最初适应得很快,从 2021 年 1 月到 2022 年 6 月,适应度每 6 个月翻一番,但从 2022 年 7 月到 2025 年 12 月,适应度每 2.4 年减慢一倍。季节性流感 H3N2 保持了更慢、更稳定的速度,每 10.0 年适应度翻一番。在这两种情况下,适应度增益率密切跟踪适应度方差,符合费舍尔基本定理的 1:1 期望。亲代和子代谱系之间的系统发育对比将大部分适应性增益定位于尖峰,并在尖峰内定位于受体结合域,其中尖峰 S1 替换的简单计数可以预测谱系适应性以及深度学习逃逸和蛋白质语言模型分数。因此,直接测量适应性为突变代理提供了一种透明的、基于频率的替代方案,用于跟踪和预测病毒适应。网站 blab.github.io/fitness-flux/ 是本文的预期阅读体验,提供响应式布局和交互式图形。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该研究调查了 季节性流感 病毒的病毒适应节奏,重点关注适应度增益率及其与 季节性流感 积累的关系。它比较了 SARS-CoV-2 和流感 H3N2,特别强调通过变异频率动态跟踪健康流量。
证据基础与方法
该综述采用直接方法通过使用多项逻辑回归分析变频动态来测量适应通量。这种方法估计每个共同循环变体的适应度,并将它们聚合起来以计算平均群体适应度的变化率。
共识与结论
研究发现,SARS-CoV-2 最初适应速度很快,从 2021 年 1 月到 2022 年 6 月,适应度每 6 个月翻一番,但从 2022 年 7 月到 2025 年 12 月,这一速度放缓至每 2.4 年一次。相比之下,季节性流感 H3N2 保持了更慢、更稳定的速度,每 10.0 年适应度翻一番。两种病毒的适应度增益率密切跟踪适应度的方差,与费舍尔基本定理的 1:1 期望一致。
争议与缺口
研究指出,虽然适应度增益率符合费舍尔定理,但 基因组 导致适应度变化的特定区域仍在调查中。该分析将大部分适应性增益定位于刺突蛋白,特别是受体结合域,但其他基因组区域的确切机制和作用尚未完全解决。
科研与实践启示
适应通量的直接测量为突变代理提供了一种透明的、基于频率的替代方案,用于跟踪和预测病毒适应。该方法可以通过更清楚地了解病毒适应度如何随时间演变而为 监测 策略、疫苗 开发和公共卫生干预措施提供信息。

Fitness flux in SARS-CoV-2 and influenza H3N2

Authors: Trevor Bedford
Original abstract (English)
The tempo of viral adaptation is usually read indirectly from the composition of mutations, through measures such as dN/dS. Here we measure it directly from the dynamics of variant frequencies, where we use multinomial logistic regression to estimate a fitness for each co-circulating variant. We aggregate these estimates to derive the rate of change of mean population fitness, referred to as fitness flux. Tracing SARS-CoV-2 from its emergence, we find that it initially adapted rapidly, doubling in fitness every 6 months from Jan 2021 to Jun 2022, but slowing to every 2.4 years from Jul 2022 to Dec 2025. Seasonal influenza H3N2 sustained a slower, steadier pace doubling in fitness every 10.0 years. In both, the rate of fitness gain closely tracks the variance in fitness, matching the 1:1 expectation of Fisher’s fundamental theorem. Phylogenetic contrasts between parent and child lineages localize most fitness gain to spike, and within spike to the receptor-binding domain, where a simple count of spike S1 substitutions predicts lineage fitness about as well as deep-learning escape and protein-language-model scores. Measuring fitness directly thus offers a transparent, frequency-based alternative to mutational proxies for tracking and anticipating viral adaptation. The website blab.github.io/fitness-flux/ is the intended reading experience of this paper, providing responsive layout and interactive figures.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study investigates the tempo of viral adaptation in seasonal influenza viruses, focusing on the rate of fitness gain and its relationship to mutation accumulation. It compares SARS-CoV-2 and influenza H3N2, with a specific emphasis on tracking fitness flux through variant frequency dynamics.
Evidence base and method
The review employs a direct method to measure fitness flux by analyzing variant frequency dynamics using multinomial logistic regression. This approach estimates fitness for each co-circulating variant and aggregates these to calculate the rate of change of mean population fitness.
Consensus and conclusions
The study finds that SARS-CoV-2 initially adapted rapidly, doubling in fitness every 6 months from Jan 2021 to Jun 2022, but this rate slowed to every 2.4 years from Jul 2022 to Dec 2025. In contrast, seasonal influenza H3N2 sustained a slower, steadier pace, doubling in fitness every 10.0 years. The rate of fitness gain in both viruses closely tracks the variance in fitness, aligning with the 1:1 expectation of Fisher’s fundamental theorem.
Controversies and gaps
The study notes that while the rate of fitness gain matches Fisher’s theorem, the specific regions of the genome contributing to fitness changes remain under investigation. The analysis localizes most fitness gain to the spike protein, particularly the receptor-binding domain, but the exact mechanisms and the role of other genomic regions are not fully resolved.
Research and practice implications
The direct measurement of fitness flux offers a transparent, frequency-based alternative to mutational proxies for tracking and anticipating viral adaptation. This method could inform surveillance strategies, vaccine development, and public health interventions by providing a clearer understanding of how viral fitness evolves over time.

🚨 突发动态与新闻 / Health News

Published: 2026-10-15  |  Publisher: GOV.UK
A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

National flu and COVID-19 surveillance report: 15 October 2026 (week 42) - GOV.UK

National flu and COVID-19 surveillance report: 15 October 2026 (week 42) - GOV.UK
新闻简报The National Flu and COVID-19 Surveillance Report for 15 October 2026 (week 42) provides an update on influenza and respiratory virus activity in England. The report indicates that influenza activity remained stable and at baseline levels, with some indicators showing decreased activity among young adults. Emergency department attendances for influenza-like-illness (ILI) remained stable and above seasonally expected levels. The number of influenza-confirmed acute respiratory infection (ARI) incidents in the community decreased compared to the previous week. However, some pharmacy vaccination data was incomplete, leading to the exclusion of results for individuals aged 65 years and over, as well as those aged 6 months to under 65 years in clinical risk groups. The report also notes that RSV activity increased and is circulating at baseline levels. The data is based on surveillance systems that monitor influenza, RSV, and other seasonal respiratory viruses, with the unified sample dataset (USD) used to calculate SARS-CoV-2 positivity rates. The report highlights the use of the moving epidemic method (MEM) and the mean standard deviation method (MSD) to define activity thresholds, with some methods being experimental and subject to future revision. The findings are grounded in data up to week 41 of 2026, between 5 October and 11 October 2026.
查看新闻五要素
时间
报告基于2026年第41周的数据,即2026年10月5日至10月11日之间的数据。发布于2026年10月15日。
地点与对象
孕妇和2至3岁儿童的数据被纳入,但应谨慎解读。由于部分数据不完整,65岁以上人群以及6个月至65岁以下临床高风险人群的结果被排除。
事件
流感活动保持稳定,处于基线水平,一些指标显示年轻成年人中的活动有所下降。流感样病例(ILI)的急诊科(ED)就诊率保持稳定,并高于季节性预期水平。社区中确认为流感的急性呼吸道感染(ARI)事件数量较前一周有所减少。
规模、影响与风险
某些年龄组的药店疫苗接种数据仍不可用或不完整,导致排除了这些年龄组的结果。由于药店中报告的疫苗接种较少,这些群体受影响的程度预计较小。
应对、状态与不确定性
由于部分数据不完整,某些年龄组的结果被排除。统一样本数据集(USD)存储了所有向SGSS、呼吸道数据集市和UKHSA实验室报告的SARS-CoV-2检测结果。报告使用移动流行病方法(MEM)和均值标准差方法(MSD)来定义活动水平的阈值。
A high priorityPublic-health news

National flu and COVID-19 surveillance report: 15 October 2026 (week 42) - GOV.UK

News Brief
The National Flu and COVID-19 Surveillance Report for 15 October 2026 (week 42) provides an update on influenza and respiratory virus activity in England. The report indicates that influenza activity remained stable and at baseline levels, with some indicators showing decreased activity among young adults. Emergency department attendances for influenza-like-illness (ILI) remained stable and above seasonally expected levels. The number of influenza-confirmed acute respiratory infection (ARI) incidents in the community decreased compared to the previous week. However, some pharmacy vaccination data was incomplete, leading to the exclusion of results for individuals aged 65 years and over, as well as those aged 6 months to under 65 years in clinical risk groups. The report also notes that RSV activity increased and is circulating at baseline levels. The data is based on surveillance systems that monitor influenza, RSV, and other seasonal respiratory viruses, with the unified sample dataset (USD) used to calculate SARS-CoV-2 positivity rates. The report highlights the use of the moving epidemic method (MEM) and the mean standard deviation method (MSD) to define activity thresholds, with some methods being experimental and subject to future revision. The findings are grounded in data up to week 41 of 2026, between 5 October and 11 October 2026.
Original source text (English)
This publication is licensed under the terms of the Open Government Licence v3.0 except where otherwise stated. To view this licence, visit nationalarchives.gov.uk/doc/open-government-licence/version/3 or write to the Information Policy Team, The National Archives, Kew, London TW9 4DU, or email: psi@nationalarchives.gov.uk . Where we have identified any third party copyright information you will need to obtain permission from the copyright holders concerned. This publication is available at https://www.gov.uk/government/statistics/national-flu-and-covid-19-surveillance-reports-2026-to-2027-season/national-flu-and-covid-19-surveillance-report-15-october-2026-week-42 This report summarises the information from the surveillance systems which are used to monitor COVID-19 (caused by severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 or SARS-CoV-2), influenza, respiratory syncytial virus ( RSV ) and diseases caused by other seasonal respiratory viruses in England. The report is based on data up to week 41 of 2026 between 5 October and 11 October 2026. The main messages of this report are: Annual reports for the 2025 to 2026 season: Influenza in the UK, annual epidemiological report: winter 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Epidemiology of COVID-19 in England (published 25 June 2026) Seasonal influenza vaccine uptake in all GP patients: annual report 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Seasonal influenza vaccine uptake in school-aged children: annual report 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Seasonal influenza vaccine uptake in frontline healthcare workers: annual report 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Influenza activity remained stable and is circulating at baseline levels, some indicators showed decreased activity among young adults. Emergency department ( ED ) attendances for influenza-like-illness ( ILI ) remained stable and above seasonally expected levels. The number of influenza-confirmed acute respiratory infection ( ARI ) incidents in the community decreased compared with the previous week. Weekly influenza vaccine uptake for the 2026 to 2027 season was reported for the first time in the week 41 report. Some pharmacy vaccination data remained unavailable or incomplete at the time of analysis (week 42). Consequently, results for those aged 65 years and over and those aged 6 months to under 65 years in clinical risk groups have been excluded from this report. This is to prevent misleading conclusions being drawn from incomplete data. Data for pregnant women and children aged 2 and 3 years is included, but should be interpreted with caution as it may also be affected by incomplete pharmacy data. The impact on these groups is expected to be smaller because it is likely that fewer vaccinations are given in pharmacies to them. All cohorts will be reinstated and statistics revised once additional data becomes available. COVID-19 activity showed mixed trends and is circulating at low levels. ED attendances for COVID-19-like illness remained stable. The number of COVID-19 confirmed acute respiratory infections ( ARI ) incidents increased compared with the previous week. By the end of week 41 2026 (week ending 11 October 2026) 23.0% of all people aged 75 years and over, and 9.9% of all people aged under 75 years with a weakened immune system, had been vaccinated with an autumn 2026 dose since 1 October 2026. RSV activity increased and is circulating at baseline levels. ED attendances for acute bronchiolitis remained stable and below seasonally expected levels. Note 1: these indicators use the moving epidemic method ( MEM ) and the mean standard deviation method ( MSD ) to define thresholds to determine their respective levels of activity. Further information on these methods can be found in Influenza surveillance in Europe: establishing epidemic thresholds by the Moving Epidemic Method and Setting thresholds to determine COVID-19 activity levels using the mean standard deviation ( MSD ) method, England, 2022 to 2024 . The MEM approach is well-established for some influenza surveillance indicators, however, for other indicators both the MEM and MSD are experimental and may be subject to future revision. Influenza laboratory surveillance and GP swabbing positivity have transitioned from using MEM to using MSD . These approaches will be considered alongside expert opinion and triangulation of other data sources. The Second Generation Surveillance System ( SGSS ) captures test result information for notifiable infectious diseases, including COVID-19 and influenza, from laboratories in England. The unified sample dataset ( USD ) stores all SARS-CoV-2 test results reported to SGSS , Respiratory DataMart and UKHSA laboratories, and is used to calculate the percentage of tests positive for SARS-CoV-2 among all SARS-CoV-2 tests conducted. As of 13 October 2026, there were a total of 1,848 COVID-19 cases identified in hospital settings in week 41, remaining stable compared with 1,847 cases in the previous week. SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19) PCR positivity in hospital settings remained stable in week 41, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 8.8% up to Sunday 11 October 2026 compared with 9.0% for the same period on the previous Sunday. Positivity rates were highest among those aged 85 years and over with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 13.5% up to Sunday 11 October 2026. This has remained stable compared with 13.1% in the same age group on the previous Sunday. Note 2: data from previous seasons is aligned by day. Note 3: testing policy and practice may change over time which can impact positivity rates, therefore comparisons over time should be interpreted with caution. Notable changes in testing policy occurred during 2022 to 2023, which are outlined in the data quality report . Note 4: the highlighted line corresponds to the age group in the subplot title, grey lines correspond to all other age groups. Influenza positivity remained stable in week 41, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 6.0% up to Sunday 11 October 2026. This is compared with 6.2% on the previous Sunday. By type, influenza A positivity remained stable in week 41, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 5.4% up to Sunday 11 October 2026. Influenza B positivity decreased in the same week, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 0.3% up to Sunday 11 October 2026. Influenza A positivity rates were highest among those aged between 18 and 24 years, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 16.6% up to Sunday 11 October 2026. This has decreased from 26.3% in the same age group on the previous Sunday. Influenza B positivity rates for the same period were highest among those aged between 5 and 11 years, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 3.1%. This has increased from 1.3% in the same age group on the previous Sunday. Due to an ongoing data quality issue resulting in more tests than usual being removed, historical positivity for influenza may be slightly lower in reports published from week 25 2026 onwards. Historical estimates will be updated once the affected data has been rectified. RSV swab-positivity based PCR test results reported through SGSS have been included as a pilot indicator from week 49 2025 onwards. RSV positivity increased in week 41, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 0.7% up to Sunday 11 October 2026, compared with 0.5% up to the previous Sunday. RSV positivity rates were highest in those aged between 0 and 4 years with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 4.6% up to Sunday 11 October 2026 in week 41. This has increased from 3.7% in the same age group on the previous Sunday. Respiratory DataMart is a sentinel laboratory-based surveillance system where participating laboratories report positive and negative test results for a number of respiratory viruses from samples primarily taken in hospital. A small proportion of primary care samples are also included in this reporting. In week 41, data is based on reporting from 9 out of the 14 sentinel laboratories. In week 41, 3,512 respiratory specimens reported through the Respiratory DataMart System were tested for influenza. There were 214 positive samples for influenza: 106 influenza A (not subtyped), 48 influenza A (H3N2), 44 influenza A (H1N1)pdm09, and 17 influenza B. Overall, influenza positivity increased slightly to 6.1% in week 41 compared with 5.6% in the previous week. In week 41, 4,567 respiratory specimens reported through the Respiratory DataMart System were tested for SARS-CoV-2. There were 383 positive samples for SARS-CoV-2. SARS-CoV-2 positivity increased slightly to 8.4% compared with 7.6% in the previous week, with the highest positivity in those aged 80 years and over at 12.4%. RSV positivity increased to 1.0%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 7.9%. Adenovirus positivity remained stable at 1.3%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 8.3%. Human metapneumovirus ( hMPV ) positivity increased to 0.3%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 0.5%. Parainfluenza positivity remained stable at 2.6%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 9.2%. Rhinovirus positivity decreased slightly to 18.1%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 34.9%. DataMart data is provisional and subject to retrospective updates. Note 5: shading represents 95% confidence intervals. This section is updated fortnightly. Data below was last updated in the week 41 report. UKHSA conducts genomic surveillance of SARS-CoV-2 lineages. This section provides an overview of circulating lineages in England, derived from data on sequenced PCR -positive SARS-CoV-2 samples in SGSS . The prevalence of UKHSA -designated lineages among sequenced cases is presented in Figure 13. To account for reporting delays, we report the proportion of lineages within COVID-19 cases that have had a sequenced positive sample between 31 August 2026 and 13 September 2026. Note that low sequencing numbers, especially within the latest reporting period, will impact the accuracy of the prevalence estimates. These most recent figures should therefore be interpreted with caution. Note that lineages will be grouped independently from their parent lineage once they reach sufficient prevalence, and may be re-grouped into their parent lineage if their prevalence subsequently falls. The data quality report contains more information on lineage groupings. Analysis of viruses from primary and secondary care shows that circulating A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses show low genetic diversity and all belong to clade D.3.1.1. Observations published from the WHO influenza vaccine composition meeting (VCM) in September 2026 suggest low reactivity of some viruses within the D.3.1.1 subclade with post-infection ferret antisera raised against A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1)pdm09-like virus – the A(H1N1)pdm09 component in 2026 Southern and 2026/2027 Northern Hemisphere cell and egg-based influenza vaccines - and human sera post-vaccination with the Southern Hemisphere vaccine 2026. It is not yet clear how vaccine effectiveness against clinical disease may be affected by these observations. Vaccines typically still provide protection against severe clinical disease when drifted strains are seen with only a modest reduction in effectiveness. The characterisation of circulating A(H3N2) viruses shows that A(H3N2) viruses are currently showing low genetic diversity. All sequenced viruses to date belong to genetic subclade K. To date, only a small number of influenza B viruses have been detected, which show some genetic diversity. Between week 35 2026 (week ending 30 August 2026) and week 41 2026 (week ending 11 October 2026), the UKHSA respiratory virus unit ( RVU ) has genetically characterised 177 seasonal influenza viruses, and identified 111 influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses, 59 influenza A(H3N2) viruses, and 7 influenza B viruses. Details of the characterised viruses by subtype are shown in Tables 1, 2 and 3. The RVU has confirmed by genome sequencing the detection of live attenuated influenza vaccine ( LAIV ) viruses in two influenza A/B positive samples, collected from children aged between 2 and 16 years. UKHSA RVU performs antigenic characterisation of influenza A(H1N1)pdm09, influenza A(H3N2) and influenza B viruses using haemagglutination inhibition ( HI ) assays. Data from these assays are used to compare how similar the currently circulating influenza viruses are to the strains included in seasonal influenza vaccines, and to monitor for changes in circulating influenza viruses. Similarity of currently circulating influenza strains to vaccine strains is defined as having an antibody titre within 4-fold when compared with reference viruses representative of the vaccine strain. Antigenic characterisation of viruses in the current reporting period is underway. Influenza positive samples are screened for mutations in the virus neuraminidase ( NA ) and the cap-dependent endonuclease of the polymerase acidic protein (PA) genes known to confer neuraminidase inhibitor (oseltamivir and zanamivir) or baloxavir resistance, respectively. Results from this surveillance are given in table 4. There have been no detections with mutations related to reduced susceptibility/resistance to oseltamivir and/or reduced susceptibility to baloxavir. Data is presented on viral ARI incidents in different settings that are reported to UKHSA health protection teams ( HPTs ). Please note that reporting practices are known to vary between seasons and between regions. Any interpretation of temporal and regional trends should consider the likelihood of differences in reporting of ARI incidents over time and between regions. FDA approves Moderna's mRNA seasonal flu vaccine - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos

News Brief
The Food and Drug Administration (FDA) approved Moderna's mRNA seasonal flu vaccine, mFLUSIVA, on August 15, 2026, for use in adults over 50. This approval comes after clinical trials showed the vaccine reduced confirmed flu cases by 27% compared to the current standard vaccine. The safety profile was similar to the standard vaccine, though more people reported mild side effects. The vaccine is expected to be available for the 2026-2027 respiratory season and may help reduce severe illness, hospitalization, and death in older adults. Moderna plans to monitor its performance this winter and submit data to the FDA to assess its suitability for people 65 and older. The approval was initially delayed but was reconsidered after Moderna committed to additional studies. The FDA's decision reflects the potential of the mRNA platform to address public health challenges through rapid production and adaptability to circulating strains.

基因组测序揭示布隆迪一所中学中隐藏的流感甲型H3N2流感病毒亚系K学校暴发 - Bioengineer.org

Genomic Sequencing Reveals Hidden Influenza A(H3N2) Subclade K School Outbreak in Burundi - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报2026年1月,布隆迪布琼布拉的一所中学出现学生呼吸道疾病病例急剧上升的情况,最初疑似由SARS-CoV-2引起。然而,SARS-CoV-2检测结果为阴性,导致诊断陷入僵局。2026年10月17日发表在《BMC Infectious Diseases》上的研究使用靶向病毒富集测序技术分析了来自学生的八份样本。结果显示,流感甲型H3N2流感病毒亚系K是导致暴发的原因,其中八份样本中有七份检测呈阳性。这四份样本提供了接近完整的基因组,从而能够进行详细特征分析。基因组分析表明,序列在系统发育树上分为两个明显不同的组,提示病毒可能存在多次引入或在社区内循环传播。这一发现突显了基因组监测在识别和理解暴发中的价值,尤其是在初始检测结果不明确的情况下。该研究还强调了严重急性呼吸道感染监测作为新兴疾病应对工具的重要性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Genomic Sequencing Reveals Hidden Influenza A(H3N2) Subclade K School Outbreak in Burundi - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
In January 2026, a secondary school in Bujumbura, Burundi, experienced a sharp increase in respiratory illness among students, initially suspected to be caused by SARS-CoV-2. However, testing for SARS-CoV-2 came back negative, leading to a diagnostic dead end. A study published on October 17, 2026, in BMC Infectious Diseases used targeted viral enrichment sequencing to analyze eight samples from the students. The results revealed that Influenza A(H3N2) subclade K was responsible for the outbreak, with seven of the eight samples testing positive. Four of these samples provided near-complete genomes, allowing for detailed characterization. The genomic analysis showed that the sequences split into two distinct groups on the phylogenetic tree, suggesting multiple introductions or community circulation of the virus. This finding highlights the value of genomic surveillance in identifying and understanding outbreaks, especially when initial tests are inconclusive. The study also underscores the importance of severe acute respiratory infection surveillance as a tool for emerging disease preparedness.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, October 17, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Genomic Sequencing Reveals Hidden Influenza A(H3N2) Subclade K School Outbreak in Burundi by October 17, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Genomic Sequencing Reveals Hidden Influenza A(H3N2) Subclade K School Outbreak in Burundi Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram In January 2026, health authorities in Burundi confronted a familiar and unsettling scenario. A secondary school in Bujumbura reported a sharp increase in respiratory illness among its students, and the immediate suspicion fell on SARS-CoV-2, the virus responsible for COVID-19. The prospect of a resurgence in a densely populated school setting was a serious concern for the national public health system. Yet when symptomatic students were tested for SARS-CoV-2, the results came back negative, leaving clinicians and epidemiologists with a cluster of unexplained severe acute respiratory infections and no identified cause. That diagnostic dead end is precisely where the investigation described in a study published in BMC Infectious Diseases begins. A team led by Néhémie Nzoyikorera of the National Reference Laboratory at the National Public Health Institute in Burundi, working with colleagues at Erasmus University Medical Center in Rotterdam and partners across East Africa, Morocco, and Germany, applied targeted viral enrichment sequencing to nasopharyngeal samples collected from symptomatic students. The approach was designed to cast a wide net: rather than testing for one pathogen at a time, the method enriches and sequences viral genetic material present in a sample, allowing researchers to identify whatever viruses are there, known or unknown. The technical foundation of the investigation rests on the Illumina Viral Surveillance Panel version 2, run on the compact iSeq 100 sequencing platform. Eight samples from SARS-CoV-2-negative students were processed through this targeted enrichment workflow. The panel uses probes designed to capture viral genomes across a broad range of respiratory and other viruses, concentrating scarce viral nucleic acid against a background of human DNA and other genetic noise. After sequencing, the reads were quality-checked, screened for viral identification, and then assembled into genome sequences where coverage permitted. Discover more Get Cold Relief Check Symptoms Order DNA Kits Study Biology Track Outbreaks The results were decisive. Influenza A virus was detected in seven of the eight samples analyzed, and four of those samples yielded near-complete influenza A genomes of sufficient quality for detailed genomic characterization. When the researchers assigned clades to the reconstructed genomes, every characterized virus fell into the same category: Influenza A(H3N2) subclade K. This was the first genomic detection and characterization of that subclade in Burundi, a finding that carries significance well beyond the school gates because H3N2 subclade K has been drawing attention internationally as an emerging variant of the seasonal influenza virus. Influenza A(H3N2) is one of the seasonal influenza A subtypes that circulates in human populations and is notorious for its capacity to evolve rapidly. The hemagglutinin protein, the HA of H3N2, is the primary target of protective antibodies, and mutations accumulating in the HA gene allow the virus to escape immunity built up through previous infections and vaccination. Clade and subclade designations, such as subclade K, are assigned on the basis of characteristic amino acid signatures in HA, and they help global surveillance laboratories track which genetic variants are spreading, which are vaccine-matched, and which signal a need for updated vaccine composition. Phylogenetic analysis of the Burundian sequences added a layer of complexity to the outbreak picture. The sequences did not form a single tight cluster. Instead, they split into two distinct groups on the phylogenetic tree, suggesting that at least two separate viral lineages of H3N2 subclade K were circulating during the outbreak. This observation matters for outbreak interpretation. A single introduction followed by onward transmission would typically produce a compact cluster of closely related genomes, whereas the presence of divergent lineages points to multiple introductions or to undetected circulation in the community before the school cluster was recognized. Genomic analysis was therefore able to reveal structure in the outbreak that routine testing alone could not. The researchers also examined the neuraminidase sequences for markers associated with resistance to neuraminidase inhibitors, the class of antiviral drugs that includes oseltamivir. No resistance-associated markers were detected, an important clinical finding because it indicates that antiviral treatment would be expected to remain effective against the circulating viruses if severe cases required it. The absence of resistance markers also provides reassurance for national treatment guidelines and for any future response to H3N2 subclade K in the region. Discover more Genomic genomic Bioengineering News Subscription Bioengineering careers guide Bioengineering Career Guide To ensure the findings contributed to the global picture, the influenza genome sequences were shared through GISAID, the Global Initiative on Sharing All Influenza Data, which serves as the central repository for influenza sequence data used by the World Health Organization and surveillance laboratories worldwide. Sharing genomes from Burundi addresses a longstanding gap in global influenza surveillance, which has historically been dominated by data from high-income countries in temperate regions. Sequences from African settings, particularly from outbreaks identified through unexplained severe acute respiratory infection surveillance, help clarify how subclade K and other variants move between regions and how they evolve in different population contexts. The study also underscores the value of severe acute respiratory infection surveillance as a pillar of emerging disease preparedness. The samples in this investigation had been collected initially for SARI surveillance, and the national ethics framework, including approval by the National Ethics Committee of Burundi with a consent waiver, allowed their use for pathogen identification. When the initial hypothesis of a COVID-19 resurgence collapsed, the surveillance infrastructure and the stored samples provided the raw material for a rapid pivot to unbiased pathogen detection. The Burundi Public Health Emergency Operation Center supported the epidemiological investigations, and reagents were provided through the East African Community Secretariat’s Mobile Lab Project, illustrating how regional cooperation underpins laboratory response capacity. The broader lesson from the Bujumbura school outbreak is that targeted viral enrichment sequencing is a practical tool for investigating acute respiratory outbreaks when the cause is unknown, particularly when initial testing is negative and routine testing for other respiratory viruses is limited. In settings where multiplex PCR panels are expensive or unavailable, a single sequencing run can identify the pathogen, characterize its genome, assign its clade, screen for resistance markers, and place it in a global phylogenetic context. The first genomic characterization of Influenza A(H3N2) subclade K in Burundi demonstrates that genomic surveillance capacity, strengthened through projects such as GREAT-LIFE at the national public health institute, can deliver actionable findings from a modest number of samples, and that the identification of distinct viral lineages within a single school outbreak highlights the value of genomic approaches to complement routine laboratory testing and support respiratory pathogen surveillance in Burundi and across the region. Time
The outbreak was reported in January 2026, with the study published on October 17, 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak occurred in a secondary school in Bujumbura, Burundi, affecting students.
Event
Genomic sequencing identified an outbreak of Influenza A(H3N2) subclade K in a Burundian school, initially suspected to be caused by SARS-CoV-2 but later ruled out through testing.
Scale, impact and risk
Seven out of eight tested samples were positive for Influenza A virus, with four yielding near-complete genomes. The outbreak involved multiple viral lineages, indicating possible community circulation or multiple introductions.
Response, status and uncertainty
The investigation used targeted viral enrichment sequencing to identify the cause, revealing the presence of Influenza A(H3N2) subclade K. The study highlights the importance of genomic surveillance in outbreak investigations.

季节性流感 疫苗 可在卫生中心获得

Seasonal influenza vaccine available at health centres
新闻简报半岛新闻社于2026年10月15日发布的卡塔尔报道指出,季节性流感 疫苗 在全国PHCC卫生中心均可获得。该疫苗 免费提供,建议所有6个月及以上年龄的人群接种,尤其是那些并发症风险较高的群体,如婴幼儿、老年人、孕妇、患有慢性病的人群以及医护人员。PHCC强调,疫苗接种不仅限于儿童时期,还包括针对多种疾病的疫苗,包括麻疹、腮腺炎、风疹、白喉、破伤风、百日咳、脊髓灰质炎、乙型肝炎、人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)、肺炎球菌病、呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)和脑膜炎球菌病。在呼吸道病毒高发季节,季节性流感 疫苗 尤其重要,因为它有助于减轻疾病严重程度、减少并发症和住院人数。PHCC的疫苗接种安排支持适当的评估和推荐剂量的及时接种,补充其传染病控制团队在疾病预防、监测、意识提升和培训方面的工作。该报道强调了疫苗接种在保护个人、保障家庭及更广泛社区方面的重要性,通过降低严重疾病和传播的风险来实现这一目标。

时间
The seasonal influenza vaccine is available free of charge at PHCC health centres across Qatar, as part of efforts to protect individuals and communities from infectious diseases. Published: 15 Oct 2026 - 08:36 am | Last Updated: 15 Oct 2026 - 12:02 pm.
地点与对象
卡塔尔,PHCC在全国运营31个卫生中心。疫苗建议所有6个月及以上年龄的人群接种,特别关注并发症风险较高的群体,包括婴幼儿、老年人、孕妇、患有慢性病的人群以及医护人员。
事件
PHCC建议所有6个月及以上年龄的人群每年接种一次疫苗接种,特别关注并发症风险较高的群体。
规模、影响与风险
在呼吸道病毒高发季节,季节性流感 疫苗尤为重要。每年接种一次疫苗接种有助于减轻疾病严重程度、减少并发症和住院人数。PHCC的疫苗接种计划涵盖针对多种疾病的疫苗,包括麻疹、腮腺炎和风疹(MMR)、白喉、破伤风、百日咳、脊髓灰质炎、乙型肝炎、人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)、肺炎球菌病、新冠状病毒(COVID-19)、呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)和脑膜炎球菌病。
应对、状态与不确定性
PHCC提供常规和季节性疫苗接种,以及针对旅行、朝觐和觐礼的疫苗,符合国家疫苗接种计划。疫苗在PHCC卫生中心免费提供,获取安排遵循各中心公布的程序。疫苗接种工作补充了PHCC传染病控制团队在疾病预防、监测、意识提升和医护人员培训方面的工作。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Seasonal influenza vaccine available at health centres

News Brief
The Peninsula news report from Qatar, published on 15 October 2026, highlights the availability of the seasonal influenza vaccine at PHCC health centres across the country. The vaccine is offered free of charge and is recommended for everyone aged six months and older, especially those at higher risk of complications, such as young children, older adults, pregnant women, individuals with chronic diseases, and healthcare workers. PHCC emphasizes that immunization is not limited to childhood and includes vaccines for various diseases, including measles, mumps, rubella, diphtheria, tetanus, pertussis, polio, hepatitis B, HPV, pneumococcal disease, RSV, and meningococcal disease. The seasonal influenza vaccine is particularly important during the respiratory virus season, as it helps reduce the severity of illness, complications, and hospital admissions. PHCC's vaccination arrangements support appropriate assessments and timely administration of recommended doses, complementing the work of its Control of Communicable Diseases Team in disease prevention, surveillance, awareness, and training. The report underscores the importance of vaccination in protecting individuals and safeguarding families and the wider community by reducing the risk of serious illness and transmission.
Original source text (English)
CHAIRMAN: DR. KHALID BIN THANI AL THANI EDITOR-IN-CHIEF: PROF. KHALID MUBARAK AL-SHAFI News Opinion Writers Videos Editorials Handbook Qatar Health General Education Government Weather Transport Culture How To? World Gulf Middle East Asia Europe Africa Americas Business Qatar Business Middle East Business World Business Stock Market Energy Sports Qatar Sport Football Formula One NBA Cricket Tennis Rally Athletics Motosports Golf Horse Racing Olympics Padel Cuesports Views Editor-in-Chief Opinion Editorial Doha Today Community Campus Souq Life Style Technology Motoring Feature & more Wellness Food Travel Fashion Science & Environment Entertainment Cinema Music In Focus Qatar 2022 Doha Expo Asian Cup IAAF Doha2019 Ramadan Qatar National Day World Cup 2026 News Opinion Writers Videos Editorials Handbook Qatar / Health Seasonal influenza vaccine available at health centres Published: 15 Oct 2026 - 08:36 am | Last Updated: 15 Oct 2026 - 12:02 pm Follow usnews Copy A+ A- The Peninsula Doha, Qatar: The seasonal influenza vaccine is available free of charge at Primary Health Care Corporation (PHCC) health centres across Qatar, as part of efforts to protect individuals and communities from infectious diseases. Vaccination remains a key pillar of disease prevention at every stage of life, from infancy to older adulthood. PHCC provides routine and seasonal vaccinations, as well as vaccines for travel, Hajj and Umrah, in accordance with the National Immunization Programme. Executive Director of Preventive Health Department at PHCC, Dr. Hamad Al Mudahka emphasised the importance of raising awareness about vaccination and its role in protecting individual and community health. He highlighted the department's commitment to promoting vaccine awareness through educational campaigns and outreach activities targeting all age groups. Manager of Health Protection at PHCC, Dr. Khalid Elawad stressed the importance of ensuring access to vaccination services and enhancing public awareness of their benefits. PHCC’s immunisation programme covers vaccines against several diseases, including measles, mumps and rubella (MMR), diphtheria, tetanus, pertussis, polio, hepatitis B, human papillomavirus (HPV), pneumococcal disease, COVID-19, respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) and meningococcal disease. Vaccine requirements depend on age, health status and approved recommendations. The seasonal influenza vaccine is particularly important during the respiratory virus season. PHCC recommends annual vaccination for everyone aged six months and older, with particular attention to groups at greater risk of complications, including young children, older adults, pregnant women, people with chronic diseases and healthcare workers. As influenza virus strains change from season to season, annual vaccination helps reduce the severity of illness, complications and hospital admissions. The vaccine is available free of charge at PHCC health centres, with access arrangements following the procedures announced by individual centres. PHCC emphasises that immunisation is not limited to childhood. While children must complete their recommended vaccination schedules, adolescents, adults, pregnant women and older people may also require vaccines according to their health conditions, risk factors, travel requirements and seasonal considerations. Children who miss scheduled vaccine doses can receive catch-up vaccinations based on their age and immunisation history. Delayed doses should not prevent parents from completing their children’s vaccination schedules. PHCC operates 31 health centres across Qatar, supporting access to healthcare and preventive services. Its vaccination arrangements also help ensure appropriate assessments and the timely administration of recommended doses. Vaccination efforts complement the work of PHCC’s Control of Communicable Diseases Team, which supports disease prevention, surveillance, awareness and healthcare professional training. These activities contribute to strengthening infection prevention and improving public understanding of communicable diseases. With the influenza season underway, PHCC encourages eligible individuals to receive their annual flu vaccine, particularly those most vulnerable to complications. Vaccination protects individuals while helping safeguard families and the wider community by reducing the risk of serious illness and transmission. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Vaccination against seasonal flu has started - vreme.com

News Brief
Vaccination against seasonal flu has begun in Serbia on October 16, 2026, with 315,000 free doses provided. The campaign targets high-risk groups such as individuals over 65, those with chronic illnesses, pregnant women, and others prone to severe complications. Doctors emphasize the importance of annual vaccination due to the changing strains of the virus, as recommended by the World Health Organization. Two vaccines, Torvaxflu and Vaksigrip, are available, with the former intended for adults and the latter for children over six months and pregnant women. The flu season is expected to start in December and peak in January and February, so vaccination is advised in November to allow time for immunity to develop. This initiative aims to create collective immunity and prevent the spread of the virus.
Original source text (English)

Add Weather to your Google feeds Photo: AP 315.000 doses of free vaccines against seasonal flu have been provided in Serbia Copied 315.000 doses of free vaccines against seasonal flu have been provided in Serbia. Doctors especially recommend immunization for people over 65 years old, chronically ill, pregnant women and other people with an increased risk of complications Vaccination against seasonal flu it started in Serbia. Doctors advise persons at particular risk of severe forms and complications of the flu to be vaccinated. There are two vaccines available, which are free. What vaccines are available Sa "Batut" Institute it was announced that a total of 315.000 doses of flu vaccines were planned and contracted for the upcoming season. Two vaccines are available to patients. Photo: Tanjug/Miloš Milivojević Vaccination against seasonal flu has started throughout Serbia In question are 288.000 doses of the trivalent vaccine "Torvaxflu" produced by the Institute for Virology, Vaccines and Serums "Torlak" and 27.000 doses of trivalent "Vaksigrip" manufactured by Sanofi Pasteur. As stated, Torlak's vaccine contains three strains, two strains of type A and one of type B, in accordance with the recommendation of the World Health Organization. This vaccine is intended for adults. In addition to adults, children older than six months and pregnant women can also receive Vaxigrip. Photo: TANJUG/MINISTRY OF HEALTH/A. ANGELOVSKI/bs One of the free vaccines was made in Torlak Flu symptoms are high fever, headache, malaise, severe muscle and throat pain, cough, etc. Doctor's recommendations Doctor Mira Alimpijević from the Belgrade Health Center tells Nova.rs that it is recommended to receive one dose of the flu vaccine every year, because the strains circulating in the population change every year, and the composition of the vaccines is different. Photo: Tanjug/Ana Paunković Doctors especially recommend immunization for people over 65 years old, chronically ill, pregnant women and other people with an increased risk of complications "That is why citizens are recommended to get a flu vaccine every year, in order to protect themselves, but also their family members, people with chronic and malignant diseases or people with immunodeficiencies. As a result of greater vaccination coverage, collective immunity is created in the population and thus the spread of the epidemic is prevented," she points out for Nova. She adds that the flu season begins in December, and that the epidemic reaches its peak in January and February. He recommends that the vaccination be done in November, because it takes two to three weeks to achieve immunity. Who should get vaccinated? Vaccination is carried out before the flu season, and is especially recommended for people with chronic diseases, people over 65, pregnant women and other groups with an increased risk of more serious complications. Photo: Pexels/Polina Tankilevitch Fever is one of the symptoms of flu "The recommendation of the World Health Organization is that active immunization should be carried out in persons at special risk. These include persons with malignant diseases regardless of the current therapeutic status, renal dysfunction, hemoglobinopathy, chronic neurological disorders, immunosuppression, extremely obese persons..." explains the doctor. When asked why it is desirable to vaccinate healthy people, the answer is that the vaccine is least effective in those for whom it is most important, and thus prevents the transmission of the virus. According to the doctor, pregnant women in the first trimester of pregnancy, people allergic to eggs, people who had an allergic reaction to the flu vaccine and people who currently have an infection, have a fever, cough, sneeze, etc. are never vaccinated. Osivax Reports Phase 2b Results for OVX836 and Advances Combination Strategy for Next-Generation Seasonal Influenza Vaccines

Osivax Reports Phase 2b Results for OVX836 and Advances Combination Strategy for Next-Generation Seasonal Influenza Vaccines
新闻简报Osivax, a clinical-stage biopharmaceutical company, announced the results of its Phase 2b field efficacy trial for OVX836, a monovalent nucleoprotein-based influenza vaccine candidate. The trial, conducted in Europe with 2,850 healthy adults aged 18–59 years, did not meet its pre-specified primary endpoint of 50% vaccine efficacy, with a point estimate of 24% (not statistically significant). However, secondary endpoints showed emerging patterns of preliminary VE signals across influenza A subtypes, with estimates of 28% (CDC), 27% (A/H3), and 25% (A/H1N1pdm09). The vaccine demonstrated a favorable safety profile and robust NP-specific antibody and T-cell responses. These findings reinforce the company's confidence in the potential of nucleoprotein-targeted immunity as a complementary mechanism to conventional seasonal influenza vaccines. Osivax is advancing a combination strategy with traditional vaccines, supported by collaborations with GC Biopharma and an undisclosed global pharmaceutical partner, aiming to develop next-generation vaccines with broader and more durable protection. The first Phase 1/2 combination study is anticipated in Q4 2027.
查看新闻五要素
时间
第二阶段b期试验结果于2026年10月15日公布,试验在欧洲进行,第一阶段1/2期联合研究预计于2027年第四季度开展。
地点与对象
试验在欧洲进行,涉及2,850名18至59岁的健康成年人。
事件
Osivax宣布了其OVX836疫苗在Phase 2b现场有效性试验中的结果,该试验未达到其预设的主要终点50% 疫苗有效性,但在不同流感A亚型中显示出初步保护效力信号的初步趋势。
规模、影响与风险
试验未达到50%的疫苗效力(VE),其点估计VE为24%(无统计学意义)。次要终点显示VE点估计分别为28%(CDC)、27%(A/H3)和25%(A/H1N1pdm09)。疫苗表现出良好的安全性,并且具有强效的针对核蛋白的抗体和T细胞免疫应答。
应对、状态与不确定性
试验结果支持OVX836与传统季节性流感 疫苗联合开发。公司正在推进联合治疗策略,并计划于2027年第四季度启动第一项Phase 1/2联合研究。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Osivax Reports Phase 2b Results for OVX836 and Advances Combination Strategy for Next-Generation Seasonal Influenza Vaccines

News Brief
Osivax, a clinical-stage biopharmaceutical company, announced the results of its Phase 2b field efficacy trial for OVX836, a monovalent nucleoprotein-based influenza vaccine candidate. The trial, conducted in Europe with 2,850 healthy adults aged 18–59 years, did not meet its pre-specified primary endpoint of 50% vaccine efficacy, with a point estimate of 24% (not statistically significant). However, secondary endpoints showed emerging patterns of preliminary VE signals across influenza A subtypes, with estimates of 28% (CDC), 27% (A/H3), and 25% (A/H1N1pdm09). The vaccine demonstrated a favorable safety profile and robust NP-specific antibody and T-cell responses. These findings reinforce the company's confidence in the potential of nucleoprotein-targeted immunity as a complementary mechanism to conventional seasonal influenza vaccines. Osivax is advancing a combination strategy with traditional vaccines, supported by collaborations with GC Biopharma and an undisclosed global pharmaceutical partner, aiming to develop next-generation vaccines with broader and more durable protection. The first Phase 1/2 combination study is anticipated in Q4 2027.

My Markets Watchlist Markets News Search Business Strategy Economy Finance Retail Advertising Careers Law Media Real Estate Small Business The Better Work Project Personal Finance Tech Science AI Enterprise Transportation Startups Innovation Markets News Stocks Indices Commodities Crypto Currencies ETFs Lifestyle Entertainment Culture Travel Food Health Education Parenting Military & Defense Politics Reviews Home Kitchen Style Streaming Pets Tech Deals Gifts Tickets Video Big Business So Expensive View From Above Small Business Authorized Account Risky Business Boot Camp Still Standing How Crime Works Life Lessons Level Up Community Games Boxed Pipeline Knighthop Tally Osivax Reports Phase 2b Results for OVX836 and Advances Combination Strategy for Next-Generation Seasonal Influenza Vaccines PRESS RELEASE GlobeNewswire Oct. 15, 2026, 05:30 AM Phase 2b study provides first consistent efficacy signal for a monovalent nucleoprotein-based influenza vaccine across both major influenza A subtypes OVX836 did not meet its pre-specified primary endpoint of 50% vaccine efficacy Results support development of OVX836 in combination with traditional seasonal influenza vaccines to provide a complementary layer of protection Strategic collaborations with GC Biopharma and a second, undisclosed global pharmaceutical partner enable next phase of combination vaccine development Lyon, France and Liège, Belgium - 15 October 2026 - Osivax, a clinical-stage biopharmaceutical company developing differentiated influenza vaccines designed to generate broad-spectrum immune responses, today announced results from its sponsored Phase 2b field efficacy trial of OVX836 and outlined the next phase of development for its next-generation seasonal influenza vaccine strategy. The Phase 2b field efficacy trial evaluated the efficacy, safety and immunogenicity of OVX836 (480 μg) versus placebo in 2,850 healthy adults in Europe. The trial did not achieve its pre-specified primary endpoint of 50% vaccine efficacy (VE), while a point estimate VE of 24% (not statistically significant) was reported. Secondary endpoints of VE, such as the CDC-defined influenza-like illness and the strain-specific VE showed an emerging pattern for a preliminary VE signal with VE point estimates of 28% (CDC), 27% (A/H3) and 25% (A/H1N1pdm09), respectively. The vaccine also demonstrated a favorable safety profile consistent with previous clinical trials and induced robust NP-specific antibody and T-cell responses. To Osivax’s knowledge, these findings represent the first consistent efficacy signal for a monovalent nucleoprotein-based influenza vaccine across both major influenza A subtypes in a field efficacy trial. In parallel to the development of OVX836 as a standalone vaccine candidate, Osivax has been advancing a combination strategy to harness the potential synergistic effects of influenza vaccines targeting different influenza antigens. The Phase 2b results further support this strategy, which combines the broad cellular immunity induced by OVX836 with the strain-specific protection provided by established seasonal influenza vaccines. Recently established collaborations with GC Biopharma and a second, undisclosed global pharmaceutical partner will enable the development of combination vaccine candidates in a single injection. This approach has the potential to substantially improve overall vaccine efficacy. “Large efficacy studies are designed not only to evaluate clinical benefit, but also to deepen our understanding of novel immune mechanisms under real-world conditions," said Dr Nicola Groth, Chief Medical Officer of Osivax. "While the study did not meet its primary endpoint, the consistency of the efficacy signal across influenza A subtypes, together with the observed immunogenicity and favourable safety profile, reinforces our confidence that nucleoprotein-targeted immunity can provide a complementary mechanism of protection alongside conventional seasonal influenza vaccines.” “These results provide a clear direction for the future development of OVX836. Rather than replacing existing seasonal influenza vaccines, we believe the greatest opportunity lies in complementing them” said Alexandre Le Vert, CEO and Co-Founder of Osivax.“By combining strain-specific antibody protection with the broad cellular immunity induced by OVX836, we aim to develop next-generation influenza vaccines capable of delivering broader and more durable protection against seasonal influenza.” The two collaborations provide access to established seasonal influenza vaccines for the development of combination vaccine candidates. Securing distinct agreements with two established leaders in influenza vaccines represents a significant milestone for Osivax and a strong external validation of its combination strategy. The first Phase 1/2 combination study is anticipated in Q4 2027. About OVX836 OVX836 is a first-in-class influenza A vaccine candidate that targets the nucleoprotein (NP), a highly conserved internal antigen. Unlike surface antigens, the NP is much less likely to mutate, providing a broader and more universal immune response. Osivax’ oligoDOMTM technology enables the design and production of a recombinant version of the NP, which self-assembles into a nanoparticle, thus triggering powerful T- and B-cell immune responses. OVX836 has been tested in 7 clinical trials with over 4,000 participants so far, and has shown promising safety, immunogenicity, and efficacy read-outs. About OXV836-005 – Asuniva Asuniva is a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, multicenter Phase 2b trial conducted in Belgium, France, Finland and Germany, to assess the safety, immunogenicity, and efficacy of a single intramuscular dose of OVX836 (480 μg) in 2,850 participants aged 18–59 years. Asuniva is the first large, field efficacy study with OVX836 which generated the clinical evidence needed to advance the vaccine toward late-stage development. Further information can be found at clinicaltrials.gov. About Osivax Osivax is a clinical-stage biopharmaceutical company developing a portfolio of differentiated influenza vaccines generating superior T-cell responses in addition to strong and sustained B-cell responses, thanks to its novel, self-assembling nanoparticle platform technology, oligoDOMTM. The company’s lead candidate, OVX836, is a broad-spectrum influenza A vaccine candidate currently in Phase 2b clinical trials with over 4,000 volunteers tested and encouraging first signal of vaccine efficacy data. By combining OVX836 with standard flu vaccines, Osivax is advancing toward a best-in-class seasonal flu vaccine to prevent all strains of influenza. Osivax collaborates with leading global health agencies, including US based BARDA and NIH, and European-based institutions and top-tier pharmaceutical companies, reinforcing its mission to prevent all types of seasonal and pandemic influenza. The company will expand into other infectious disease indications through combinations and collaborations worldwide. For further information: www.osivax.com Contact Alexandre LE VERT, CEO contact@osivax.com +33 (0)9 70 30 13 80 For Media Inquiries Trophic Communications Anja Heuer - osivax@trophic.eu +49 (0) 151 106 199 05 Attachment Osivax Reports Phase 2b Results for OVX836 and Advances Combination Strategy for Next-Generation Seasonal Influenza Vaccines Markets Insider and Business Insider Editorial Teams were not involved in the creation of this post. 季节性流感疫苗尚未分发。- KOHA.net

Seasonal flu vaccines have not yet been distributed. - KOHA.net
新闻简报KOHA.net于2026年10月15日发布的新闻报道指出,季节性流感疫苗尚未在科索沃分发,预计到达日期推迟至2026年10月21日。这一延迟归因于2026/2027流感季节推荐的流感病毒株变化以及制造商的问题。普里什蒂纳的主要家庭医学中心和其他医疗机构正在等待关于疫苗供应情况的进一步信息。每年接种疫苗的市民表达了对延迟的担忧,因为这影响了他们获得保护的能力。医生们指出流感样症状的患者数量有所增加,但提醒这些症状可能由其他病毒引起。卫生部表示正在采取必要措施应对延迟,并将追究经济运营商的责任。目前情况仍在调查中,未明确提及具体风险等级。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该新闻报道于2026年10月15日发布。事件日期未明确说明,但疫苗预计于2026年10月21日到达,流感季节即将来临。
地点与对象
该事件与科索沃共和国有关,特别提到首都普里什蒂纳的主要家庭医学中心以及一般人口,包括每年接种疫苗的市民。
事件
季节性流感疫苗尚未在科索沃分发,疫苗接种的安排已被推迟。延迟归因于流感病毒株的变化以及制造商的问题。国家公共卫生研究所(NIPHK)和卫生部已承认延迟,并正在等待进一步信息。
规模、影响与风险
疫苗分发延迟导致依赖年度疫苗接种接种的市民产生不确定性。医生报告称流感样症状的患者数量增加,但这些症状可能由其他病毒引起。影响规模未量化,但延迟被指出影响整个人口。风险未明确说明,但情况被描述为正在调查中。
应对、状态与不确定性
国家公共卫生研究所和卫生部正在等待关于疫苗及其分发情况的进一步信息。延迟是由于病毒株变化和制造商问题所致。目前情况仍在调查中,卫生部表示将采取必要措施追究导致延迟的经济运营商责任。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Seasonal flu vaccines have not yet been distributed. - KOHA.net

News Brief
The news report from KOHA.net, published on October 15, 2026, highlights that seasonal flu vaccines have not yet been distributed in Kosovo, with the expected arrival date pushed to October 21, 2026. This delay is attributed to changes in the recommended influenza virus strains for the 2026/2027 season and issues with the manufacturer. The Main Family Medicine Center in Pristina and other healthcare providers are awaiting further information about the vaccine's availability. Citizens who are vaccinated annually are expressing concern over the delay, as it affects their ability to receive protection. Doctors note an increase in patients with flu-like symptoms, though they caution that these could be caused by other viruses. The Ministry of Health has stated that it is taking necessary actions to address the delay and will hold the economic operator accountable. The situation remains under investigation, with no explicit risk level mentioned.

By: Arianita Hoti 15 October 2026 19:23 Share to: By: Arianita Hoti 15 October 2026 19:23 Share to: As the flu season approaches, the number of patients with flu symptoms has begun to increase at Family Medicine Centers, while citizens who are vaccinated every year still do not know when they will be able to receive the vaccine this season. Doctors say they expect an increased number of patients. The Main Family Medicine Center in the capital does not have information about when the vaccinations will begin. Citizens who are vaccinated every year against seasonal flu are still waiting for their vaccines, while they have no information on when they will be able to receive them this season. Adem Krasniqi says that vaccination helps him against the flu and that he is looking forward to getting it this year as well. "Yes, I've received it every year. Only this year I don't know if they'll start giving it or not, because I'm receiving them, they're really helping," said Krasniqi. Even Artan Sinanaj, who gets vaccinated every year, says he doesn't know when he will be able to get it. "I get the seasonal flu vaccines every year and I've had results, not just me, but my whole family, with parents, with children, with everyone. More or less, we think it's making things easier for us, normally, because we get them for that purpose too. Realistically, I haven't gotten them this year yet, maybe they haven't even arrived yet," he said. also Arberi October 1, 2026 Seasonal flu vaccines will arrive in Kosovo three weeks late Vaccination was scheduled to begin in early October, but the National Institute of Public Health has announced that the vaccines will arrive in Kosovo three weeks late. The Main Center for Family Medicine in Pristina said they are awaiting information about vaccination against seasonal flu. Family doctor, Rushit Ismajli, says that so far they have not received any notification from the National Institute of Public Health, neither about vaccines nor about the start of vaccination. "We still do not have any information from the National Institute of Public Health about the seasonal flu, but we are waiting to have both the information and the vaccine for this situation," Ismajli said. He stressed that in recent days, an increase in the number of patients seeking medical help for flu-like symptoms has been observed, although there have been no complicated cases. "We have had a slightly increased number of patients with flu-like symptoms, with cough, fever, runny nose, body aches, but these can also be related to other viruses, not necessarily to seasonal flu. They can be related to a nasal virus, which we call rhinovirus. We also expect to have an increased number of patients this season, so we are also prepared as staff to deal with these situations," said Ismajli. According to the National Institute of Public Health, the delay in the arrival of vaccines is related to changes in the influenza virus strains recommended for this season. also Arberi 10 o. më parë Hoti: Seasonal flu vaccines arrive in Kosovo this week "This year, the international influenza vaccine production process has been affected by changes in the composition of the recommended strains for the northern hemisphere vaccine. These changes have affected production and supply deadlines at the global level, as well as the supply of countries in the region, including the Republic of Kosovo, where the vaccines are expected to arrive 21 days later than planned. Seasonal influenza vaccines for the 2026/2027 season are expected to arrive in the Republic of Kosovo on October 21, 2026," the announcement made earlier by the National Institute of Public Health (NIPHK) states. According to the National Institute of Public Health, 85 doses of the vaccine are expected to be delivered this season. The Ministry of Health has announced that the supply of the seasonal flu vaccine is being delayed due to problems with the manufacturer. According to the Ministry of Health, the economic operator with which it has an active contract notified the Ministry of the delays at the beginning of this month. "The Ministry of Health has taken all necessary actions in a timely manner to secure and supply the seasonal flu vaccine. However, the delays in this case were caused by the manufacturer itself, for which the Ministry was notified by the economic operator. For the delays caused in the supply, the Ministry of Health will take the necessary measures against the economic operator, in accordance with the terms of the contract and the legal provisions in force," the Ministry announced. Unlike Kosovo, the Ministry of Health and Social Protection in Albania has announced the launch of seasonal flu vaccination on September 30 and the provision of 280 doses, which will be available to priority groups. Last year, flu vaccination in Kosovo also began on October 1. also Arberi 21th of 2026 NIPHK: Vaccination against seasonal flu is expected to begin in October According to the NIPHK, about one billion cases of seasonal influenza are reported each year worldwide, while about 3 to 5 million of them present in severe forms. 2026流感季你需要知道的一切

Everything you need to know about flu season 2026
新闻简报The article discusses the early onset of flu season 2026 in the U.S., with flu activity climbing since early September and the latest FluView report released on October 9. Region 9, which includes Arizona, California, Hawaii, and Nevada, has shown an exceptionally high positivity rate of 9.5 percent, compared to a nationwide rate of 3.6 percent. This is a sixfold increase from the 0.6 percent positivity rate in 2025. The dominant strain in high-positivity areas is H1N1, a subtype of influenza A, with 98.4 percent of positive tests indicating this strain. H3N2, another influenza A subtype, accounts for 1.6 percent of positive tests. Experts note that the current H1N1 strain may differ from the vaccine strain, potentially affecting vaccine effectiveness. The CDC advises vaccination before flu activity peaks, typically in December or January, and recommends getting the shot as early as possible, especially in areas already seeing spikes. The article highlights the importance of monitoring the situation and evaluating the impact of antigenic drift on vaccine performance and public health risks.
查看新闻五要素
时间
流感活动自9月初以来持续上升,最新发布的FluView报告时间为10月9日。美国流感季通常在秋季开始加速,高峰期在12月至2月之间。当前流感季比往常更早开始,某些地区在10月初已出现激增。
地点与对象
这是地点,特别提到地区9(包括亚利桑那州、加利福尼亚州、夏威夷州和内华达州)流感阳性率较高。受影响的人群包括所有年龄段,其中H1N1主要影响年轻人群,而H3N2历史上主要影响老年人群。
事件
2026流感季比往常更早开始,某些流感病毒株,尤其是H1N1,显示出活动性增加。美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)报告称,与往年相比,流感的阳性率显著上升,且高阳性区域中占主导地位的病毒株是H1N1,属于甲型流感病毒的一种。
规模、影响与风险
The nationwide percent positivity rate for influenza is 3.6 percent, a sixfold increase compared to 2025. Region 9 has a positivity rate of 9.5 percent, which is exceptionally high for this time of year. The CDC's data show that 98.4 percent of positive tests indicate H1N1, and 1.6 percent indicate H3N2. The risk of a severe winter flu season is still uncertain, and the effectiveness of the current vaccines is under evaluation.
应对、状态与不确定性
专家正在密切关注局势,CDC提供更新的数据。建议接种流感疫苗疫苗以进行防护,最佳接种时间是在流感活动达到高峰前(通常在12月或1月)。当前疫苗疫苗(包括新批准的mRNA 疫苗)的有效性仍在评估中,同时正在讨论抗原漂移对疫苗效果的影响。

Explore Conditions Cardiovascular health Digestive health Endrocrine system Hearing Mental health Recent articles Guides More More Wellness Your body Conditions Guides Advertisement Advertisement Advertisement Trending: Protein myths dietitians want to bust Cozy slippers that don't skimp on support The surprising link between food and sleep Hair-growth hype experts aren't buying Health Conditions Infectious Flu Everything you need to know about flu season 2026 An early start doesn’t necessarily mean disaster Scientific American Follow Claire Maldarelli Fri, October 16, 2026 at 4:00 PM UTC The beginning of fall and back-to-school time means cold virus season has arrived. Usually, colds hit as lesser-evil infections before influenza season takes over. But already, the flu is popping up early in pockets of the U.S. Here's why that might be and everything else you need to know as we head into the dreaded annual flu season. According to the Centers of Disease Control and Prevention's latest FluView report, released on October 9, flu activity has been climbing since early September. Flu season in the U.S. generally begins gaining momentum in the fall, with activity peaking between December and February. But surveillance indicators show that certain strains of influenza are rising fast. The CDC's latest data show a nationwide percent positivity rate for influenza—the percentage of all positive influenza tests—of 3.6 percent. That's a sixfold increase compared with this time in 2025, when the rate was 0.6 percent. Parts of the U.S. are showing much higher positive rates. The CDC splits the country up into regions, and Region 9, which includes Arizona, California, Hawaii and Nevada, had 9.5 percent positivity, according to the latest data. Experts say that is exceptionally high for this time of year. "It's not unheard of to see things rising at this stage," says Andrew Pekosz, an immunologist at the Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health. "But it's also something that we probably shouldn't be seeing until maybe November or December at the earliest." Flu can be caused by different types of influenza, including influenza A (and its subtypes) and influenza B. Usually, flu season brings a mix of types. Right now, in the areas of the country with the highest positivity rates, H1N1, a subtype of influenza A, is dominant. According to the CDC, the most recent weekly testing data showed that 98.4 percent of the positive tests indicated H1N1 and that 1.6 percent revealed H3N2, another influenza A subtype. Still, experts say it's too early to predict whether these spikes could signal a particularly severe winter flu season. Will this year's flu vaccines work well? One of the best ways to protect yourself from the flu is to get a flu vaccine. Every year experts reformulate these vaccines depending on what strains of the virus are circulating or predicted to circulate during the coming winter season. Advertisement Advertisement Whichever subtype dominates matters because it can help predict which groups will experience the greatest disease burden. It can also determine how well existing immunity will protect people, as well as how the year's flu vaccines will perform. H1N1 and H3N2 are both influenza A subtypes, but they are evolutionarily distinct and can affect people differently. H3N2-dominant seasons have historically been associated with particularly severe illness among older adults. H1N1, on the other hand, has had a greater effect on younger populations—but both can cause serious disease at any age. "The version [of H1N1] that's circulating seems to be a little bit different from the vaccine strain that we have and from H1N1 strains that have circulated before. It's what we call an antigenic drift," Pekosz says. That could be why the virus is emerging so early, he adds. If it can evade prior immunity, the pathogen will be easier to transmit. But it's still too early to know how that might influence the effectiveness of the current flu vaccines. When is the best time to get vaccinated? Trish Perl, a professor of internal medicine and a specialist in infectious disease at UT Southwestern Medical Center, says that right now is the best time to get a flu shot. Advertisement Advertisement "Immunity from the flu vaccine is strongest for about four to six months, and we usually expect flu to peak in December or January," Pekosz says. "So a September or October vaccination will get you through the worst part of the flu season." Perl agrees—she says that she often tells her people to, at the very least, get a flu vaccine before Thanksgiving. Because after that, she says, people have unlimited excuses for why they can't find the time. And with certain areas already seeing spikes, getting the shot earlier than usual may be best, Pekosz adds. "The best time to get vaccinated is before you see influenza in your area," he says. What about the newly approved mRNA vaccine for flu? On August 5, 2026, the Food and Drug Administration approved Moderna's mFLUSIVA, the first mRNA seasonal influenza vaccine, for adults aged 50 and older. In a phase 3 clinical trial, the vaccine was found to be 26.6 percent more effective at preventing the flu than a standard flu shot. Advertisement Advertisement FDA approval and inclusion in federal vaccination recommendations—informed by guidance by the CDC's Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices (ACIP)—are separate regulatory steps, however. The disruption to ACIP under the Trump administration has made incorporating newly approved vaccines into guidance more complicated. Moderna had said the vaccine would be available at select retailers during the 2026–2027 flu season. But so far, access appears to be limited. Experts, including Pekosz and Perl, say that if people who qualify for the new mRNA vaccine have access to it, they should consider it. "The data would suggest that if you can get the mRNA vaccine, you probably will get a stronger response to most of the components in the vaccine than if you get the standard one. But again, the standard one is pretty good as well," Pekosz says. He and Perl are optimistic about the potential of mRNA technology to improve flu vaccines in the future. Because mRNA vaccines can be developed and manufactured more quickly than traditional shots, scientists could wait until later in the year before selecting which influenza strains to target. That added time could help us better predict which strains will circulate during flu season—ultimately meaning more effective vaccines. Advertisement Advertisement The best thing anyone can do right now is to get their flu shot as soon as possible. And if you do end up getting sick, Perl says not to forget about antivirals, such as Tamiflu, which can be very effective and, in many cases, could reduce the amount of time you are infectious. View five news elements
Time
Flu activity has been climbing since early September, with the latest FluView report released on October 9, and flu season in the U.S. generally begins gaining momentum in the fall, peaking between December and February. The current flu season is starting earlier than usual, with certain areas already seeing spikes in early October.
Location and population
is the location, with specific mention of Region 9, which includes Arizona, California, Hawaii, and Nevada, having a high positivity rate for influenza. The affected population includes all age groups, with H1N1 affecting younger populations and H3N2 historically affecting older adults.
Event
Flu season 2026 has started earlier than usual, with certain strains of influenza, particularly H1N1, showing increased activity. The CDC reports that the positivity rate for influenza has risen significantly compared to previous years, and the dominant strain in high-positivity areas is H1N1, a subtype of influenza A.
Scale, impact and risk
The nationwide percent positivity rate for influenza is 3.6 percent, a sixfold increase compared to 2025. Region 9 has a positivity rate of 9.5 percent, which is exceptionally high for this time of year. The CDC's data show that 98.4 percent of positive tests indicate H1N1, and 1.6 percent indicate H3N2. The risk of a severe winter flu season is still uncertain, and the effectiveness of the current vaccines is under evaluation.
Response, status and uncertainty
Experts are monitoring the situation closely, with the CDC providing updated data. The flu vaccine is recommended for protection, and the best time to get vaccinated is before flu activity peaks, typically in December or January. The effectiveness of the current vaccines, including the newly approved mRNA vaccine, is still under assessment, and the implications of antigenic drift on vaccine performance are being discussed.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

即将到来的流感季会有多严重? - Gavi,疫苗联盟

How bad will the upcoming flu season be? - Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance
新闻简报来自Gavi,疫苗联盟的文章讨论了即将到来的北半球流感季的预期严重程度,并从刚刚结束的南半球流感季中获得了一些见解。文章指出,南半球的流感季与往年相比总体上保持一致,被描述为中度。科学家们正在密切关注H1N1流感病毒的小变化,这些变化可能会影响北半球的流感季。主要流行的病毒是熟悉的季节性毒株,包括H1N1和流感B Victoria系。世界卫生组织报告称,2026年2月至8月期间,全球流感检测数量比2025年同期有所减少。尽管有这些观察结果,即将到来的流感季的严重程度仍不确定,而疫苗接种被强调为预防严重疾病的关键。弗朗西斯·克里克研究所的研究人员正在进行进一步的测试,以评估具有不同感染和疫苗接种病史的人群的抗体对新兴流感毒株的识别能力。文章强调,尽管南半球的经验提供了一些安慰,但它并不能保证北半球会有类似的结果。
查看新闻五要素
时间
南半球的流感季现在正在结束。
地点与对象
南半球国家如澳大利亚、新西兰和南非。北半球国家。
事件
南半球的流感季预计为中度,尽管预测较为困难。科学家们正在监测H1N1流感病毒的小变化,这些变化可能会影响即将到来的流感季。主要流行的病毒是熟悉的季节性流感毒株,包括H1N1和流感B Victoria系。世界卫生组织报告称,2026年2月至8月期间,全球流感检测数量比去年同期有所减少。
规模、影响与风险
即将到来的北半球流感季预计为中度。在流感季开始前,其严重程度难以预测。疫苗接种预计将在减少流感负担方面发挥关键作用。2026年2月以来,H3N2病毒在南半球最为常见,其中亚群K占分析的H3N2病毒的大多数。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究人员正在进行进一步的测试,以评估具有不同感染和疫苗接种病史的人群的抗体对新兴流感病毒的识别能力。世界卫生组织和疾病控制与预防中心正在监测这一情况并提供指导。即将到来的流感季的严重程度难以预测,而疫苗接种仍然是预防严重疾病的关键。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

How bad will the upcoming flu season be? - Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance

News Brief
The article from Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance, discusses the expected severity of the upcoming northern hemisphere flu season, drawing insights from the recently concluded southern hemisphere season. It notes that the southern hemisphere’s flu season was broadly in line with previous years and is described as moderate. Scientists are closely monitoring small changes in H1N1 influenza viruses that could influence the northern season. The main circulating viruses were familiar seasonal strains, including H1N1 and the influenza B Victoria lineage. The World Health Organization reported fewer influenza detections globally between February and August 2026 compared to the same period in 2025. Despite these observations, the severity of the upcoming season remains uncertain, and vaccination is highlighted as a key defense against severe disease. Researchers at the Francis Crick Institute are conducting further tests to evaluate how well antibodies from people with varying infection and vaccination histories recognize emerging flu strains. The article emphasizes that while the southern hemisphere’s experience offers some reassurance, it does not guarantee a similar outcome for the northern hemisphere.
